## 65. [Criptoativos e soberania nacional: desafios da regulação tecnológica do sistema financeiro digital brasileiro.](<https://doi.org/10.69849/d2qkve35>)

- 作者：Fábio Dinoá Buriti
- 发表日期：2026-11-11
- 引用次数：0
- DOI：https://doi.org/10.69849/d2qkve35
- [打开 PDF](<https://www.revistaft.com/ft/article/download/3076/8101>)

**摘要：**

The growth of the crypto-asset market challenges state authority in financial matters, particularly because transactions on decentralized networks cross borders and may operate without traditional intermediaries. This article examines how the Brazilian legal system can regulate crypto-assets without undermining national sovereignty, legal certainty, and freedom of enterprise. The research is qualitative, bibliographical, and documentary, based on the 1988 Federal Constitution, Law No. 14,478/2022, Decree No. 11,563/2023, Central Bank regulations, and studies published between 2020 and 2025, alongside relevant earlier theoretical works. The findings indicate that regulation does not remove technological decentralization but can govern service providers, establish transparency duties, and strengthen the prevention of unlawful activities. Constitutional protection also requires institutional coordination, clear information for users, and risk-proportionate supervision. The article concludes that financial sovereignty in the digital environment depends less on blocking innovation than on building effective, updated rules compatible with fundamental rights.

---

## 66. [Blockchain Technology and Financial Services Transformation: Evidence from Institutional Cryptocurrency Adoption](<https://doi.org/10.22146/abis.v14i4.125051>)

- 作者：Dasrat Rai Meghwar
- 发表日期：2026-11-11
- 引用次数：0
- DOI：https://doi.org/10.22146/abis.v14i4.125051
- [打开 PDF](<https://journal.ugm.ac.id/abis/article/download/125051/46001>)

**摘要：**

Introduction/Main Objectives: The advent of blockchain technology has introduced a new paradigm in the financial landscape through decentralization, immutability, transparency and enhanced efficiency in financial services. This study focuses on institutional cryptocurrency adoption and its implications for financial services transformation in the blockchain era. Background Problems: Most studies have focused on the institutional adoption of one cryptocurrency or one institutional investment channel. This study focuses on institutional cryptocurrency adoption via three distinct institutional investment channels for Bitcoin and four other key cryptocurrencies. Novelty: There have been some limited studies that have attempted to quantify the various institutional channels for investing alongside the key cryptocurrencies. This study set out to fill this gap through a thorough empirical assessment of institutional cryptocurrency adoption. Research Methods: The study adopted a quantitative method and secondary data was obtained from credible databases and market reports on cryptocurrencies. Descriptive analysis and comparative concentration of ownership analyses were used on the basis of participation of institutions, geographical coverage, institutional holdings, CR5, CR10, Herfindahl Hirschman Index (HHI) and Gini coefficient. Finding/Results: These results highlight the patterns of institutions in adopting digital assets, specifically Bitcoin, as part of the broader digital currency adoption in today's financial environment. Blockchain technology is helping in the modernization of financial services. Conclusion: The paper empirically quantifies institutional cryptocurrency adoption through different investment channels and digital assets on the market, which contributes to the literature. The findings are provided attention to the financial service industry, institutional investors, policymakers and market regulators.

---

## 67. [Social Influence as a Moderator of Cognitive Biases and Market Sentiment in Digital Gold Investment: Tring! Users in Denpasar](<https://doi.org/10.61132/jepi.v4i4.2835>)

- 作者：I Gusti Ayu Wina Aura; Gusi Putu Lestara Permana
- 发表日期：2026-11-11
- 引用次数：0
- DOI：https://doi.org/10.61132/jepi.v4i4.2835
- [打开 PDF](<https://journal.areai.or.id/index.php/jepi/article/download/2835/2439>)

**摘要：**

Digital gold micro-investment platforms have made gold accessible to retail investors, yet investment decisions on these platforms are not driven by rational fundamentals alone. This study examines the effect of heuristic behavior, market sentiment, and overconfidence bias on digital gold investment decisions, and tests whether social influence moderates these relationships among Tring! application users in Denpasar. A quantitative, causal-associative design was applied using a five-point Likert-scale questionnaire distributed through an online survey to respondents selected by purposive sampling (domiciled in Denpasar, active Tring! users for at least six months, and with a steady income). The data were analyzed using Partial Least Squares Structural Equation Modeling (PLS-SEM) with SmartPLS. The results show that heuristic behavior, market sentiment, and overconfidence bias each have a positive and significant effect on digital gold investment decisions, and that social influence significantly strengthens all three relationships. The model explains 67.3% of the variance in investment decisions and shows strong predictive relevance (Q²predict = 0.588). These findings highlight the role of the social environment in amplifying the psychological drivers of investment behavior in digital micro-investment ecosystems.

## 69. [An Exploration of Kindergarten to 12th Grade Teachers’ Morals and Decision-Making for Technology: A Phenomenological Study](<https://digitalcommons.liberty.edu/doctoral/9033>)

- 作者：Nishi Shairs
- 发表日期：2026-11-11
- 引用次数：0
- [打开 PDF](<https://digitalcommons.liberty.edu/context/doctoral/article/10092/viewcontent/2_Shairs_2C_20Nishi_20_28L32541778_29.pdf>)

**摘要：**

The purpose of this transcendental phenomenological study was to explore teachers’ moral decision-making and technology for K-12 teachers in the United States. The theory that guided this study was Bandura’s social cognitive theory of moral thought and action, which describes the importance of moral engagement or disengagement in the decision-making process. The central research question for this study was, “What are the lived experiences of kindergarten-12th grade teachers that describe their engagement or disengagement of morals when making decisions with technology in the classroom?” The research design was a transcendental phenomenological design using Moustakas (1994) transcendental phenomenological steps for analysis. The participants were 10 K-12 teachers who have used integrated technology within a school setting. Data collection was conducted through individual interviews, artifact analysis, and document analysis of oral journal entries. Data analysis identified five themes: (1) Student Wellbeing, (2) Dishonesty, (3) Absolute Truth, (4) AI Concerns in the Classroom, and (5) Teacher Use of Technology for Efficiency.

## 70. [Cryptanalysis of Isogeny-Based Quantum Money with Rational Points](<https://doi.org/10.22331/q-2026-11-10-2263>)

- 作者：Hyeonhak Kim; Donghoe Heo; Seokhie Hong; Sangjin Lee; Suhri Kim
- 发表日期：2026-11-10
- 引用次数：0
- DOI：https://doi.org/10.22331/q-2026-11-10-2263
- [打开 PDF](<https://quantum-journal.org/papers/q-2026-11-10-2263/pdf/>)

**摘要：**

Quantum money is a representative application in cryptography that utilizes quantum technology to achieve security that is unattainable with classical systems. Since the theoretical proposal by Wiesner in 1983, the most recent instantiation is the quantum money using class group actions on elliptic curves, proposed by Montgomery and Sharif in Asiacrypt'24. In this work, we propose a concrete cryptanalysis by leveraging the efficiency of evaluating division polynomials with the coordinates of rational points, offering a speedup of O ( log 4 ⁡ p ) compared to the brute-force attack. While the resulting attack still runs in exponential time, our method provides the first nontrivial algorithmic insight into their structural hardness. Furthermore, due to the algebraic properties of rational points and quadratic twists, our technique naturally yields a more efficient verification procedure for the underlying quantum money. We believe this new line of analysis opens promising directions for future research in elliptic-curve-based quantum cryptography.

---

## 71. [Property rights in virtual things: digital files revisited](<https://doi.org/10.1080/17579961.2026.2718577>)

- 作者：Johan David Michels; Christopher J. Millard
- 发表日期：2026-11-10
- 引用次数：0
- DOI：https://doi.org/10.1080/17579961.2026.2718577
- [打开 PDF](<https://www.tandfonline.com/doi/pdf/10.1080/17579961.2026.2718577?needAccess=true>)

**摘要：**

In this paper, we develop a proposal for an in rem property right in digital files by analogy to things in possession. Under our proposal, English law would recognise a control-based title to digital files, backed by a remedy for return or damages that protects a computer user’s control of a digital file from third-party interference. This would subject third parties to a duty not to interfere with another person's control of a digital file deliberately, such as by editing or deleting the file. This duty would arise whenever a third party had sufficient notice of another computer user’s exclusive claim to the file. By contrast, this duty would not be breached by acts which did not interfere with another user’s control of a file, such as merely accessing or copying the information a file contained. In this manner, the law can achieve an appropriate balance between competing interests in digital files.

## 72. [Islands of Exception: Law, Empire, and Offshore Finance in the Caribbean](<https://doi.org/10.1017/9781009656504>)

- 作者：Jose Atiles
- 发表日期：2026-11-10
- 引用次数：0
- DOI：https://doi.org/10.1017/9781009656504
- [打开 PDF](<https://www.cambridge.org/core/services/aop-cambridge-core/content/view/E93A327F8B9804F3B6B5E7FB5FA576EF/9781009656511AR.pdf/islands-of-exception.pdf>)

**摘要：**

This Element explores the colonial foundations and imperial design of offshore finance, arguing that tax havens are not anomalies but central to global capitalism. Centering the Caribbean and Puerto Rico, it shows how colonial legality and geopolitical subordination produced zones marked by a logic of inclusive exclusion, where secrecy, corporate power, and tax injustice are normalized. Drawing on TWAIL and law and political economy, the Element introduces the concepts of colonial state of exception and the corporate citizen as key legal formations of the colonial offshore economy. It examines how secrecy, financial services, and fintech enable corporations to externalize harm and evade accountability, and how tax reforms, like the OECD's Pillar Two and the UN Tax Convention, often reproduce colonial and racialized hierarchies. The Element concludes with a call to abolish the colonial offshore economy and to uplift grassroots movements across the Caribbean that demand transparency, sovereignty, and justice.

---

## 73. [Can Generative AI Preserve Tail-Risk Networks? Evidence from Cryptocurrency Markets](<https://doi.org/10.21203/rs.3.rs-11498528/v1>)

- 作者：Jun Sun; Ruting Wang; Junda Wu
- 发表日期：2026-11-10
- 引用次数：0
- DOI：https://doi.org/10.21203/rs.3.rs-11498528/v1
- [打开 PDF](<https://www.researchsquare.com/article/rs-11498528/latest.pdf>)

**摘要：**

Abstract Generated financial scenarios inform risk estimation and portfolio decisions, but agreement with standard distributional diagnostics does not show that a generator preserves the structure a decision depends on. We evaluate generated scenarios in three layers: conventional distributional and risk diagnostics; preservation of a conditional lower-tail network, judged against the sampling uncertainty of the empirical network; and realised outcomes of an allocation rule that uses the network’s node rankings. Using hourly data for ten cryptocurrencies over 2020–2025, we compare a stationary bootstrap, a GARCH-t copula model and a denoising diffusion probabilistic model (DDPM). The layers yield different conclusions about these generators. The discrepancy between the aggregated GARCH-t network and the observed network exceeds the bootstrap reference band at all 16 evaluation refits, yet overall distributional discrepancy stays within its band in 96 of the 157 GARCH-t panels that fall outside both the conventional-diagnostic and network bands. DDPM meets the joint conventional-diagnostic criterion in no panel but meets the single-panel network criterion in 120 of 160. Network differences change allocation weights and volatility exposure. After ex ante risk matching, differences in daily expected shortfall relative to the historical-network rule range from −0.09 to −0.02 percentage points, and every paired 95% interval contains zero. Controlled simulations show when ranking errors are costly: if tail hubs differ mainly in asymmetric downside dependence, a correlation-matching copula reverses the hub ranking and raises population expected shortfall by 3.1% relative to the true-network benchmark. Conventional diagnostics, network preservation and allocation outcomes give distinct assessments of these generators. JEL classification: C45 · C58 · G11 · G32

## 74. [Economias de Guerra e Grupos Insurgentes: um estudo sobre o Estado Islâmico-Khorasan (ISIS-K)](<https://doi.org/10.26792/rbed.v13i1.75538>)

- 作者：Aureo Toledo; Letícia Buzá Ferreira
- 发表日期：2026-11-10
- 引用次数：0
- DOI：https://doi.org/10.26792/rbed.v13i1.75538
- [打开 PDF](<https://rbed.abedef.org/rbed/article/download/75538/42286>)

**摘要：**

Como economias de guerra contribuem para a sustentação de grupos insurgen-tes? Utilizando o Estado Islâmico-Khorasan (ISIS-K) como estudo de caso, o artigo analisa como intervenções externas e vácuos de poder contribuíram para criação de sistemas econômicos que financiam a violência política organizada. Argumentamos que uma economia de guerra formada por tais circunstâncias condicionou a atuação do ISIS-K, fornecendo recursos mediante tributação coercitiva, tráfico de opioides, mineração e redes transnacionais de doadores. A trajetória do ISIS-K demonstra também que a disponibilidade de recursos permitiu a implementação de um modelo de recrutamento baseado em incen-tivos financeiros e salários elevados, mas que se combinam com elementos de natureza ideológica e simbólica. Os resultados indicam que o grupo não apenas explora a economia de guerra local, mas a retroalimenta, criando um ciclo onde a violência se torna o próprio mecanismo de sustentação material. Assim, suge-re-se que esforços de construção da paz serão insuficientes se não desarticula-rem variáveis econômicas que tornam a guerra um sistema rentável.

## 75. [Tinjauan Hukum Ekonomi Syariah terhadap Penggunaan Kartu Sicashless sebagai Alat Pembayaran Digital di Pondok Pesantren Al-Amanah Al-Gontory](<https://doi.org/10.61132/jiesa.v3i6.2759>)

- 作者：Kresnawan Dzaki; Khoirunnisa Khoirunnisa; Achmad Dzikri Rajuli
- 发表日期：2026-11-10
- 引用次数：0
- DOI：https://doi.org/10.61132/jiesa.v3i6.2759
- [打开 PDF](<https://ejournal.areai.or.id/index.php/JIESA/article/download/2759/2296>)

**摘要：**

Digital technology has encouraged educational institutions, including Islamic boarding schools, to adopt cashless payment systems. Al-Amanah Al-Gontory Islamic Boarding School uses the SiCashless card to support students’ transactions and financial administration. This study examines the implementation, benefits, and challenges of SiCashless and analyzes its practice from the perspective of Islamic Economic Law. It employs a qualitative descriptive approach with a case-study design. Data were collected through observation, interviews, and documentation involving boarding-school administrators, system managers, students, and parents or guardians. Data analysis consisted of data reduction, presentation, and conclusion drawing, while source and method triangulation were used to assess validity. The findings indicate that balances can be added through transfers by parents to a Bank Muamalat account connected to the system or through cash top-ups under the applicable procedures. Funds are recorded according to their purposes, and transactions can be monitored through an application. The system facilitates daily transactions, reduces the need to carry cash, and supports expenditure monitoring. Challenges include internet disruptions, delayed data synchronization, balances that do not immediately appear, and lost cards. In principle, SiCashless reflects trustworthiness, transaction-record transparency, public benefit, fairness, and protection of wealth. Nevertheless, clearer contractual arrangements, transparent and reasonable administrative fees, and well-defined refund procedures are needed to strengthen sharia compliance and legal certainty.

## 77. [Crypto Assets on the Balance Sheets of U.S. Public Companies: Financing Sources, Corporate Motives, and the Evolution of Treasury Strategies](<https://doi.org/10.18778/0208-6018.377.02>)

- 作者：Piotr Międlar
- 发表日期：2026-11-10
- 引用次数：0
- DOI：https://doi.org/10.18778/0208-6018.377.02
- [打开 PDF](<https://czasopisma.uni.lodz.pl/foe/article/download/31432/30385>)

**摘要：**

This article examines the crypto assets reported on the balance sheets of U.S.-listed public companies from August 2020 through June 2026 and the sources used to finance their acquisition. The study addresses three questions: how firms finance crypto-asset purchases, which motives they disclose for holding these assets, and how corporate crypto-asset treasury strategies have evolved. Its purpose is to connect the asset side of the balance sheet with the liability and equity structure used to fund acquisitions. The exploratory study draws on filings with the U.S. Securities and Exchange Commission (SEC), including Current Reports on Form 8-K, Annual Reports on Form 10-K, Quarterly Reports on Form 10-Q, and securities prospectuses. The purposive sample comprises 18 major holders in four economically distinct classes; the financing analysis is limited to eleven firms that acquired crypto assets. The study combines descriptive statistics, content analysis of disclosed motives, and comparative case studies. Three findings emerge. First, equity issuance was used by nine of the eleven acquiring firms, whereas debt was never the sole financing source. For the six dedicated crypto-asset treasury companies for which value-based estimates were available, common equity, including in-kind contributions, accounted for 77.0% of funds raised, preferred stock for 10.5%, and debt for 12.5%. Second, firms’ behaviour during the market downturn was associated with their liability structure. Asset sales accompanied by debt reduction occurred when firms faced non-discretionary payment obligations and limited access to new issuance, whereas firms financed predominantly with equity held their positions or continued to purchase assets when access to issuance remained open. Third, later disclosures referred to an asset-accumulation business motive more often than to a precautionary motive. The practice evolved through five phases, and the 2025–2026 market downturn did not reverse its institutionalisation. The findings indicate an association between financing structure and firm behaviour, not a causal relationship. The principal limitations are the small and concentrated sample, the qualitative nature of the inference, and the attribution of financing sources at the firm-year rather than transaction level.

---

## 78. [Cosmotechnical Central Processing Unit (CCPU)](<https://doi.org/10.1145/3829214.3839390>)

- 作者：Rongxing Li; Tobias Klein
- 发表日期：2026-11-10
- 引用次数：0
- DOI：https://doi.org/10.1145/3829214.3839390

**摘要：**

CCPU: Cosmotechnical Central Processing Unit is a fully functional bitcoin-mining computer whose cooling apparatus serves as both its sculptural body and conceptual center. Its custom heat sink is composed of over one hundred stacked metal lamellae, pierced by copper heat pipes and shaped after the silhouette of a Chinese Taihu scholar rock. Each lamella is covered with thermochromic film, allowing the processor's heat to appear as subtle shifts of iridescent blue and green across the surface. CCPU uses an endoscopic camera to monitor changes in the hue of its thermochromic surface and regulate fan speed accordingly. In this feedback loop, visible material transformation becomes operational data: the machine cools itself by watching the changing color of its sculptural body. The work brings together two contrasting systems of value: the energy-intensive extraction of cryptocurrency and the contemplative tradition of the scholar's rock, historically appreciated as a miniature landscape for reflection. By turning the computer's hidden thermal labor into a visible, functional material process, CCPU exposes its energetic condition through an object that must continually dissipate heat to continue operating. Drawing on Yuk Hui's concept of cosmotechnics, the project asks how computation might be understood through culturally specific relations among matter, energy, perception, and moral order. CCPU reframes the scholar's rock not as a static symbol, but as a working technological object through which extraction, heat, feedback, and contemplation are materially entangled.

---

## 79. [Curvature of Cross-Chain Value Transport: A Gauge-Invariant Framework, Proofs, and a Pre-Registered Computational Test of Bitcoin–Ethereum Consistency](<https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.24110073>)

- 作者：Ahmed Hala
- 发表日期：2026-11-10
- 引用次数：0
- DOI：https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.24110073

**摘要：**

Value moves between Bitcoin and Ethereum through exchanges, decentralized exchanges and custodial bridges. We ask whether that transport is path-independent, and which network, liquidity and agent conditions break it. The first version of this work framed the question as a diffeomorphism between two basins of attraction; here it is replaced by objects that can be computed. Assets held at locations form a cell complex whose log-exchange rates are a 1-cochain. We prove that path independence is equivalent to exactness of that cochain (zero holonomy on every loop). We use the weighted combinatorial Hodge decomposition to split inconsistency into curl (single-venue loops) and harmonic (cross-chain loops) parts, and show the minimal Bitcoin–Ethereum complex has two harmonic dimensions. We prove two bounds on the sensitivity of the recovered price potential, and show numerically that under liquidity weighting the static sensitivity stays bounded (gain 0.85–0.88 over six decades of the Fiedler value λ2). The role of λ2 is instead dynamic: under linear arbitrage relaxation, mean-square inconsistency scales as∑︁ k 1/λk, slope −0.90 against λ2 in our graph. A reproduction gate recovers the trade probability 1/(1 + η) of Milionis, Moallemi and Roughgarden within seed spread at all five tested values of η. We also prove that mispricing collapses onto η for any block-arrival law, confirmed for Poisson blocks (p = 0.53) and fixed slots (p = 0.99). A 1,300-run, 14-factor Resolution V fractional factorial on an agent-based model finds strong cross-operator interactions (F = 28.7 on 36 terms; BIC prefers interactions) and negative curvature. Inventory and exchange depth dominate the tear, and high bridge fees remove about 40% of the benefit of inventory. The capacity-weighted tear metric is confounded by its own weights, flipping the sign of four effects. On daily data from January 2020 to May 2026, no test of a deterministic Bitcoin–Ethereum map beats both an IAAFT and a DCC-GARCH null. Hypotheses that need multi-venue tick data are registered but untested.

---

## 80. [Co‐Movements of High‐Order Moments and Risk Spillovers in the Time‐Frequency Domain Along the Supply Chains of the US Beef and Pork Industries](<https://doi.org/10.1002/agr.70183>)

- 作者：Sophia Siori; Dimitrios Panagiotou; Panos Fousekis
- 发表日期：2026-11-10
- 引用次数：0
- DOI：https://doi.org/10.1002/agr.70183
- [打开 PDF](<https://onlinelibrary.wiley.com/doi/pdfdirect/10.1002/agr.70183>)

**摘要：**

ABSTRACT The present analysis examines the multiscale co‐movements of high‐order moments, namely skewness and kurtosis, across the US beef and pork marketing channels. Monthly farm, wholesale, and retail prices, spanning January 1980 to February 2026, are employed. The results show that in the beef sector, co‐movements in the farm‐wholesale pair are strongly synchronized in medium horizons, with the wholesale leading the upstream transmission of risk to producers. Co‐movements in the beef wholesale‐retail pair are demand‐driven, with the retail level leading volatility dynamics. In the pork sector, the farm‐wholesale pair records significant negative co‐movements in asymmetry, with wholesale taking the leading role in recent years, indicating that crash risk is only weakly transmitted from packers to producers. The pork wholesale‐retail pair reported the weakest co‐movement across all moments and frequencies. To this end, we found that risk spillovers vary between scales and moments and are not uniformly spread across the two supply chains. Patterns depend significantly on both the industry (beef or pork) and the vertical level examined. Results suggest that wholesalers and retailers are likely to have a high degree of leverage over the cattle/hog producers.

## 81. [The Energy Cost of a Hash Trial: Proof-of-Work Priced from the Thermodynamic Floor Up](<https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.24102658>)

- 作者：William Alexander Aldred
- 发表日期：2026-11-10
- 引用次数：0
- DOI：https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.24102658

**摘要：**

Proof-of-work priced from the thermodynamic floor up. One double-SHA-256 trial costs 1.5e-11 J on frontier hardware (Antminer S21 Pro, 15 J/TH); the strict Landauer floor at 300 K is 7.35e-19 J per trial (256-bit erasure only), and even a generous floor counting every gate evaluation as a full bit erasure is 8.22e-15 J. Network totals, per-block energy at the 10 November 2026 difficulty, and the two regimes of efficiency improvement (fixed difficulty versus post-retarget equilibrium) are computed from cited inputs by the accompanying script. Model calculations are labelled simulated, not measured.

- [打开 PDF](<https://www.compendiumpaperasia.com/index.php/cpa/article/download/1256/648>)

**摘要：**

Cryptocurrency has emerged as an alternative digital payment method; however, its adoption for retail transactions remains limited in Malaysia. This study examines the factors influencing consumers' intention to use cryptocurrency payments by extending the Technology Acceptance Model (TAM) with social influence and perceived risk, while examining the moderating effect of digital literacy. Data were collected through a cross-sectional survey of 375 respondents in the Klang Valley who were familiar with cryptocurrency and analysed using Partial Least Squares Structural Equation Modelling (PLS-SEM). The results indicate that perceived usefulness and social influence have significant positive relationships with consumers' intention to use cryptocurrency payments, whereas the relationship between perceived ease of use and intention to use is not statistically significant. Although perceived risk showed a significant relationship with intention to use, the relationship was opposite to the hypothesised negative direction and was therefore H4 not supported. The findings further reveal that digital literacy significantly moderates the relationships of perceived usefulness and perceived ease of use with intention to use. Specifically, digital literacy weakens the relationship between perceived usefulness and intention to use while strengthening the relationship between perceived ease of use and intention to use. However, it does not significantly moderate the relationships of social influence or perceived risk with intention to use. This study contributes to the technology adoption literature by demonstrating that digital literacy serves as an important boundary condition in shaping consumers' adoption of cryptocurrency payments. The findings also provide practical implications for policymakers, fintech service providers, and educators by highlighting the importance of improving digital literacy, enhancing platform usability, and increasing consumer awareness to encourage the responsible adoption of cryptocurrency payment systems in Malaysia.

## 83. [A Separação Saudável e Necessária Entre Estado e Dinheiro](<https://doi.org/10.30800/mises.2026.v14.1677>)

- 作者：Fabio Langer Pereira

O dinheiro surgiu espontaneamente no mercado para facilitar as trocas voluntárias, mas foi progressivamente apropriado pelos governos, que estabeleceram o monopólio da emissão monetária. A resenha analisa a crítica de Rothbard a esse processo, sob a perspectiva da Escola Austríaca, abordando suas consequências econômicas e sociais, como a perda do poder de compra da moeda. Por fim, discute o Bitcoin como alternativa descentralizada e o desafio que representa ao monopólio estatal, retomando o debate sobre a separação entre Estado e dinheiro.

## 84. [AI crypto trading bots: what machine learning and LLMs can and can't do](<https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.24114944>)

- 发表日期：2026-11-10
- 引用次数：0
- DOI：https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.24114944

**摘要：**

AI trading bots and LLM agents tested on six years of Binance data: what machine learning predicts, what trading fees eat, and why LLM backtests lie. Machine learning finds a real edge in crypto prices, but a small one: gradient boosting calls the direction of Bitcoin's next hour right 53.9% of the time, and an ordinary trader's fees eat all of it. What it forecasts well is volatility, which sizes positions and keeps the risk of a strategy predictable, and that is where professional firms use it. Language models have shown no trading skill in any test long and clean enough to tell, and in backtests on history they remember what came next. An agent that holds keys is a security problem before it is a trading strategy. 63%: hit rate an hourly Bitcoin strategy needs to cover a 10 bp round trip 1.56: Sharpe ratio of the best of 20,000 random strategies that know nothing 60.0%: right on Bitcoin's monthly direction a year or more before the models' release; 46.8% after This is a PDF of the article first published at https://xmarva.github.io/blog/2026/ai-crypto-trading/, where it may since have been updated. The code and measurements behind it are at https://github.com/xmarva/transformers-from-scratch/tree/main/articles/crypto\_trading.

## 86. [Criptoativos e cadeia de custódia da prova](<https://doi.org/10.1590/scielopreprints.18789>)

- 作者：Diogo Erthal Alves da Costa
- 发表日期：2026-11-10
- 引用次数：0
- DOI：https://doi.org/10.1590/scielopreprints.18789
- [打开 PDF](<https://preprints.scielo.org/index.php/scielo/preprint/download/18789/39382/40380>)

**摘要：**

O crescimento do mercado de criptoativos e sua popularização no Brasil trouxeram esses ativos para o centro de demandas judiciais, especialmente na persecução patrimonial. Ainda assim, falta teoria consolidada sobre o tratamento probatório das evidências a eles relacionadas. Este artigo examina a aplicação da cadeia de custódia, prevista nos artigos 158-A a 158-F do Código de Processo Penal, às provas envolvendo criptoativos. O marco teórico é a concepção racional da prova. Sustenta-se que a cadeia de custódia se relaciona à fiabilidade do elemento probatório, e não à sua admissibilidade. Por isso, sua inobservância não gera nulidade e deve ser considerada no momento da valoração. A tese central é que a prova sobre criptoativos não constitui categoria unitária: suas modalidades apresentam problemas epistêmicos e jurídicos próprios e se distinguem pelo grau de autonomia do dado em relação a mecanismos externos de verificação. Os dados gravados em blockchains públicas suficientemente descentralizadas se autenticam por simples conferência pública, o que afasta a lógica da cadeia de custódia. Nos dados fornecidos por prestadoras de serviços de ativos virtuais centralizadas, a fiabilidade depende da idoneidade institucional da fonte e da forma de recebimento e manutenção do dado. Nas ferramentas de inteligência de blockchain, o agrupamento de endereços exige validação metodológica da heurística empregada. Já a atribuição desses endereços a entidades do mundo real depende de evidências off-chain, cada uma submetida ao seu próprio regime de custódia. Por fim, quando os dados sobre criptoativos estão vinculados a outras provas digitais, a cadeia de custódia tem aplicação plena e exige cautelas adicionais no manuseio de chaves privadas e frases de recuperação (seed phrases).

## 87. [PayBench DIM-04 Half B: conformance checks on five test-network rails, data pre-registration](<https://doi.org/10.17605/osf.io/bmt8p>)

- 作者：Michael Blake
- 发表日期：2026-11-10
- 引用次数：0
- DOI：https://doi.org/10.17605/osf.io/bmt8p

**摘要：**

This registration fixes, before it executes, a confirmatory run of binary conformance checks on five payment rails used by autonomous software agents, the measured annex of PayBench's DIM-04 structural profile. The structural profile states from vendor documentation what each rail's documents say about outcome certainty; this run tests a subset of those documented statements by making payments on public test networks only (Base Sepolia, Stellar Testnet, Solana devnet, Tempo Moderato and Spark's hosted regtest), with tokens that carry no monetary value, Everyday AI Ltd (the company) being the only payer and the only payee. Seven checks are registered, six conformance checks and one descriptive observation outside the conformance count: an identical resubmission cannot settle twice (two checks, one for each protocol family); a fresh signature inside the validity window (descriptive); an artefact presented after its deadline fails conclusively and its death is observable to the payer; a duplicate nonce cannot settle twice; an interrupted flow resolves to a terminal state readable by the payer and value never moves twice; and a settlement outcome reported after a settle that cannot succeed agrees with the network on value. They run as 47 cells, each a statement about one pinned test deployment and the company's own stack, never about a rail in production. Each repetition becomes one binary outcome under a fixed rule, with void rules for harness faults; each cell reports k of K valid repetitions (K = 10, or 20 where the exploratory repetitions disagreed), with its void and not-exercised counts by cause and whether it is complete. Counts only: no interval is computed, and no count is set beside another rail's as the same measurement, since each cell tests a different documented bound on a different software stack. The design was frozen and anchored after declared exploratory runs, whose data is not reused as outcome data, and before the confirmatory run or any publication; the order of the confirmatory repetitions is drawn from the hash of a Bitcoin block mined after the freeze. This registration makes no commitment to publish results.

## 88. [A Business Intelligence Application for Assessing the Environmental Impacts of Proof-of-Work Cryptocurrencies](<https://doi.org/10.3390/fintech5040105>)

- 作者：Mohammad Alhur; Raed Musbah Alqirem; Abdel Nasir Yousuf Abdelkareem
- 发表日期：2026-11-10
- 引用次数：0
- DOI：https://doi.org/10.3390/fintech5040105

**摘要：**

Proof-of-Work (PoW) cryptocurrencies create electricity demand globally, but their carbon consequences depend on where the electricity is generated. This study presents CryptoCarbonApp, an open-source R Shiny business intelligence application that expresses network-level energy and emissions estimates for four PoW cryptocurrencies through the electricity system of Jordan, whose generation mix averages about 0.39 kgCO2/kWh. The analysis reports network totals with conditional sensitivity ranges and examines an assumed spatial allocation of mining activity with a 20,000-replication Monte Carlo procedure. On Jordan’s mix, Bitcoin’s network demand corresponds to about 66 MtCO2 per year (50–83 MtCO2 for a ±25% change in network consumption); because this demand is roughly eight times Jordan’s national electricity supply, the figure is an intensity benchmark, not a forecast. A 50 MW facility would emit about 154 ktCO2 per year. The allocation procedure cannot identify actual mining hotspots, and raising the renewable share of supply from 10% to 60% reduces emissions by 55.6% under static accounting. At the September 2025 hashprice, efficient hardware breaks even at about USD 0.12/kWh before other costs, below Jordan’s business tariff of USD 0.169/kWh. Energy labelling at supervised virtual asset service providers is therefore proposed as a conditional, demand-side complement to mining-side instruments.

---

## 89. [A Comprehensive Framework for Targeted Transfer Learning: Privacy-Preserving, Secure, and Efficient Domain Adaptation](<https://digitalcommons.odu.edu/ece_etds/622>)

- 作者：Sayyed Farid Ahamed
- 发表日期：2026-11-10
- 引用次数：0
- DOI：https://doi.org/10.25777/e3fd-gd70
- [打开 PDF](<https://digitalcommons.odu.edu/context/ece_etds/article/1622/viewcontent/_Sayyed__PhD_Dissertation.pdf>)

**摘要：**

Machine learning models deployed in tactical environments rely on high-quality labeled datasets, which are often challenging, expensive, and time-consuming to obtain. In addition, formal techniques for assuring model performance under dynamic conditions remain limited. Transfer learning has emerged as a promising solution, but its effectiveness is limited by domain coverage and similarity between source and target datasets. This dissertation presents a comprehensive, modelagnostic Targeted Transfer Learning (TTL) framework that addresses transferability challenges between domains with minimal data, demonstrating how targeted sample selection improves adaptation across image classification, network security, and privacy-preserving collaborative settings. First, we develop the TTL framework for scenarios where direct access to datasets is restricted. This metadata-driven approach applies to a combinatorial coverage method to estimate domain correlation and guide sample selection. Evaluation on a satellite imagery dataset showed that targeted selection outperformed zero-shot transfer and random selection while conserving computational resources. Second, we investigate the TTL framework in settings where both datasets are directly available. This data-driven approach uses optimal transport distance to quantify distributional differences and guide selection of informative target samples. Validation across a broad set of benchmark image classification datasets demonstrated consistent improvements over conventional transfer learning. Third, we address network malware classification through Selective Targeted Transfer Learning (STTL), projecting source and target traffic data into a shared latent feature space and selecting relevant samples using a Kullback–Leibler divergence-based similarity measure. Experimental results across multiple attack-family scenarios showed that STTL achieved higher performance than conventional transfer learning, confirming that targeted selection generalizes across data types and domains. Finally, we extend the TTL framework through two decentralized privacy-preserving mechanisms. In a federated learning setting, clients share only summary statistics, and a server computes aggregation weights that enable knowledge transfer without exposing local data. In a blockchain-based setting, cryptographic verification secures model weights throughout the transfer process, while a smart-contract mechanism rewards high-quality participation. Both mechanisms consistently outperformed non-targeted baselines while preserving data confidentiality and integrity. This research establishes targeted transfer learning as a practical framework that enables machine learning models to adapt reliably, securely, and efficiently across image classification, network security, and distributed collaborative environments.

---

## 90. [The temporal connecting factor: Private international law after the Clarity Act and MiCA](<https://doi.org/10.20318/cdt.2026.10929>)

- 作者：Cayetana Santaolalla Montoya
- 发表日期：2026-11-10
- 引用次数：0
- DOI：https://doi.org/10.20318/cdt.2026.10929
- [打开 PDF](<https://e-revistas.uc3m.es/index.php/CDT/article/download/10929/8108>)

**摘要：**

A digital asset can be born as one thing and become another. The US Clarity Act follows the asset throughout its life and is built to adapt. MiCA, by contrast, fixes the asset’s nature when it is created. Neither law asks the conflict-of-laws question: which law governs an asset whose legal nature changes over time? This article compares the two models from private international law, sets out the problem their divergence creates, and only then turns to doctrine. Building on the classical search for the seat of the legal relationship, from Savigny to Rabel, it builds a theory of the temporal connecting factor and addresses characterisation, party autonomy, overriding mandatory rules, extends the analysis to jurisdiction, recognition and enforcement, and closes with concrete proposals for the HCCH-UNIDROIT Project.

---

## 91. [Digital Trade Financing and SMEs Export Intensity in Economic Community of West African States (ECOWAS)](<https://doi.org/10.67693/bjcr-z9xq6b88>)

- 作者：OMALI JOSHUA UGBEDE; OBAYORI JOSEPH BIDEMI
- 发表日期：2026-11-10
- 引用次数：0
- DOI：https://doi.org/10.67693/bjcr-z9xq6b88
- [打开 PDF](<https://bexfordpublishing.co.uk/journals/bjcr/articles/BEX_OCT_26_129/download>)

**摘要：**

This study examined the impact of digital trade financing on the performance of Small and Medium Enterprises export intensity in the Economic Community of West African States region (proxy for Developing Economies) over the period 2019 to 2024.The study employed panel data from fifteen ECOWAS member and utilized a robust methodological framework comprising Hausman specification tests, random effects estimations, Difference Generalized Method of Moments, and System Generalized Method of Moments estimators to address issues of endogeneity, unobserved heterogeneity, and dynamic panel bias.The Hausman test results found that, random effect is appropriate hence the application of GMM instrument.The Difference GMM results confirmed strong persistence in export intensity with lagged dependent variable coefficient 0.190627 and probability value 0.0000.The J-statistics for GMM estimations were effectively zero, confirming instrument validity.The study concludes that, digital trade financing exerts significant and heterogeneous effects on SME export intensity performance in the ECOWAS region.Digital standby letters of credit showed negative effects on export intensity.Dynamic discounting strongly supports digital transactions and exports.Internet penetration consistently enables export intensity.The study recommends that, policymakers prioritize digital standby letter of credit development, accelerate digital infrastructure investments and support continued research on digital trade finance and SME export intensity in the ECOWAS region.

## 93. [Personal innovativeness, attitude to government policy, and electric vehicle adoption in Indonesia: Mediation and moderation effects](<https://doi.org/10.59953/paperasia.v42i5b.1344>)

- 作者：Nadia Nila Sari; Mohd Fazli Mohd Sam
- 发表日期：2026-11-10
- 引用次数：0
- DOI：https://doi.org/10.59953/paperasia.v42i5b.1344
- [打开 PDF](<https://www.compendiumpaperasia.com/index.php/cpa/article/download/1344/669>)

**摘要：**

Despite the presence of environmental concerns, including pollution and global warming, Indonesia continues to encounter difficulties in the adoption of Electric Vehicle (EV) products. Government policy has been identified as playing a crucial role on supporting the adoption of EV. However, a paucity of implementation has been observed, which has resulted in a reluctance among the public to adopt EV. The present study examines the role of social influence, environmental concern, price, and technology on the intention to adopt electric vehicles in which attitude towards government policy acts as a mediating variable and personal innovation acts as a moderating variable. The utilization of quantitative methods, employing the Structural Equation Model Partial Least Square (SEM PLS) Software version 4, facilitates the data processing to ascertain the influence of independent, dependent, mediating, and moderating variables. The data collection in this research involved the utilization of purposive sampling with sample size of 300 respondents. The findings of this study demonstrate that price, attitudes towards government policy, personal innovativeness, social influence, and technological advancement have a significant impact on the adoption intention of electric vehicles. Moreover, the findings also demonstrate that environmental concern and price influence the attitude of government policies that mediate their influence on adoption intention. This study hypothesises that personal innovativeness functions as moderating effect between social influence and adoption intention regarding electric vehicles (EVs). Contrary to the findings of numerous other studies, this research posits that social influence exerts the predominant influence on the adoption intention. This study also emphasizes consumer attitudes towards the role of government in terms of providing subsidies, tax reductions, and other forms of support that have the potential to positively influence consumer intention to adopt electric vehicles. It is therefore suggested that company and government prepare a better strategy to develop EV adoption. This research will provide the company with valuable insights into the EV industry and government to formulate more effective policies and strategies to promote the adoption of electric vehicles (EVs) in the future. Keywords: Social Influence, Environmental Concern, Price, Technology, Attitude to Government Policy, Personal Innovativeness, Adoption Intention, Electric Vehicles

---

## 94. [ARCADIA : Adaptive Real-time Cross-chain Abuse Detection through Inductive Analysis](<https://doi.org/10.1145/3862592>)

- 作者：Babu Pillai; Aravinda S. Rao; Marimuthu Swami Palaniswami; Vallipuram Muthukkumarasamy
- 发表日期：2026-11-10
- 引用次数：0
- DOI：https://doi.org/10.1145/3862592
- [打开 PDF](<https://dl.acm.org/doi/pdf/10.1145/3862592?download=true>)

**摘要：**

Cross-chain systems were once praised for enabling seamless blockchain interoperability; however, they are now exploited for money laundering and hiding digital assets. This reveals a significant gap in decentralized compliance and oversight, primarily resulting from the use of obfuscation techniques. This paper introduces a novel Adaptive Real-time Cross-chain Abuse Detection through Inductive Analysis (ARCADIA) framework, designed to identify exploitation of cross-chain systems for illicit purposes. It uses a hybrid machine learning (ML) architecture that adapts to analyse addresses (with and without history), leveraging temporal features and graph-based inductive reasoning. Our approach enables Virtual Asset Service Providers (VASPs) to deploy ML models for real-time risk label generation. Extensive experiments on Ethereum address datasets have demonstrated superior performance in identifying phishing and malicious entities, even under severe data imbalance conditions. Our framework narrows the gap between blockchain forensics, ML, and compliance, offering a scalable solution aligned with emerging regulatory standards such as the Financial Action Task Force (FATF) Travel Rule.

---

Cada fichero lleva su sello OpenTimestamps (.ots), anclado en la cadena de bloques de Bitcoin (bloques 975346 y 975350). La huella prueba que los ficheros no cambiaron; el sello y este depósito prueban que existían el 10/11/2026, no antes. Las fechas de cada hipótesis las anotó el propio proyecto en su historial; este depósito no sustituye a un prerregistro externo hecho antes de entrenar. ## 97. [Deep learning for cryptocurrency price forecasting: A taxonomy, benchmark, and gap analysis](<https://doi.org/10.15276/hait.09.2026.35>)

- 作者：未提供

**摘要：**

Relevance.Cryptocurrency returns are volatile, non-stationary, and heavy-tailed.Purpose.This study develops a unified taxonomy and benchmark with reproducible scoring.Tasks.Representative architectures are compared and measured limitations are separated from proposed remedies.Methods.Ten statistical, linear, recurrent, attention-based, selective state-space, and pretrained models are evaluated on daily Bitcoin/Tether and Ethereum/Tether data from two thousand eighteen to two thousand twenty-six.Three expanding-window folds use horizons of five, ten, and twenty days.A target-history-only control shares post-warm-up calendar rows and normalization.Evaluation uses absolute error, symmetric percentage error, normalized-return directional agreement, and paired tests corrected for temporal dependence and multiple comparisons.Scientific novelty.The benchmark combines architectural coverage, aligned information-set control, and a zero forecast reference.Practical significance.It informs accuracy and resource trade-offs under weak predictive signal.Results.Best absolute-error skill over zero is approximately zero.Bitcoin autoregressive and recurrent model differences are not supported after correction.The recurrent model leads learned-model Ethereum mean absolute error, with many, but not all, pairwise advantages supported.Directional agreement remains near zero point five.TimesFM is the strongest native-input foundation reference; larger Chronos scale gives no consistent gain.Lag-Llama produces over-dispersed forecasts, and its low symmetric percentage error does not establish superior accuracy.Conclusions.This low-signal benchmark supports protocol-specific comparisons rather than exploitable return forecasts.Coverage is limited to two assets, three folds, and zero-shot foundation evaluation.

## 98. [Repertoires of Contention: Building computational tools and infrastructures for community resistance to bitcoin mining](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.13527>)

- 作者：Marina Johnson-Zafiris; Owen Marshall; Jen Liu; Joseph Ferdinando; Steven J. Jackson; Christopher Csíkszentmihályi
- 发表日期：2026-11-10
- 引用次数：0
- DOI：https://doi.org/10.48550/arxiv.2611.13527
- [打开 PDF](<https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.13527>)

**摘要：**

Recent work in human-computer interaction and allied fields has sought to engage community members as co-designers, facilitators, and decision makers to challenge structural power barriers and knowledge gaps. In this paper, we apply a diverse set of computational techniques to engage and address community concerns surrounding the socio-environmental impacts of the Greenidge Bitcoin mining power plant facility in upstate New York. Through collaborative interactions with community groups, addressing problems ranging from regulatory inertia to thermal water pollution, we aim to facilitate not just the production of new and critical environmental knowledge but also new computationally-supported repertoires of contention: means of contesting the unequal relations of knowledge and power that structure and often determine the outcomes of environmental controversies. We argue such engagements can help support more politically engaged forms of HCI, oriented towards efforts to redress and repair disparities in wealth, knowledge, and influence through strategic sociotechnical interventions and alliances.

---

## 99. [Percepciones sobre desarrollo, seguridad y cooperación regional: una reflexión constructivista desde la movilidad académica en El Salvador y su contraste con Colombia](<http://hdl.handle.net/11634/74704>)

- 作者：Steilan Daniela Villegas Alsina
- 发表日期：2026-11-10
- 引用次数：0

**摘要：**

Este trabajo analiza las percepciones sobre el desarrollo, la seguridad y la cooperación regional en El Salvador y su relación con el contexto colombiano, tomando como punto de partida la experiencia vivida durante la movilidad académica. El objetivo principal es comprender cómo las ideas, las experiencias y las percepciones de una sociedad pueden influir en la manera en que un país entiende su realidad y toma decisiones. Para desarrollar el análisis se tomó como base el enfoque constructivista de las Relaciones Internacionales y se utilizó una metodología cualitativa y comparativa, apoyada en la revisión de documentos, informes institucionales, fuentes académicas y datos estadísticos. A lo largo del trabajo se abordaron los cambios en materia de seguridad en El Salvador, la adopción del Bitcoin como una apuesta por la innovación económica y los mecanismos de cooperación regional, estableciendo una comparación con Colombia. El análisis permitió identificar que la imagen que proyecta un país y la forma en que sus ciudadanos interpretan los cambios sociales y políticos tienen importancia en sus relaciones internacionales. También se encontró que los avances en seguridad y desarrollo deben analizarse teniendo en cuenta sus resultados y las condiciones económicas, sociales e institucionales de cada país. Finalmente, se concluye que Colombia y El Salvador pueden encontrar oportunidades de aprendizaje mutuo mediante el diálogo y la cooperación, reconociendo sus diferencias y evitando adoptar modelos sin considerar las necesidades de cada sociedad.

---

## 100. [Conceptualising and operationalising kinship-based organised crime: a scoping review](<https://doi.org/10.1007/s12117-026-09633-4>)

- 作者：Inge-Mai A. Jønsson; Sanez Zolghadriha
- 发表日期：2026-11-09
- 引用次数：0
- DOI：https://doi.org/10.1007/s12117-026-09633-4
- [打开 PDF](<https://link.springer.com/content/pdf/10.1007/s12117-026-09633-4.pdf>)

**摘要：**

Abstract Kinship-based organised crime (KBOC) has attracted growing media attention in Northern Europe but remains under-researched in the academic literature. To address this gap, a scoping review was conducted to systematically map how KBOC has been conceptualised and operationalised across diverse geographical, cultural, and research contexts, thereby identifying commonalities, variations, and limitations in existing approaches to the study of KBOC. The analysis identified eight recurring themes through which KBOC is conceptualised and operationalised in the existing literature: intergenerational transmission of crime, concentration of criminal activity, overlap between familial and criminal structures, intermarriage between criminally involved families, marginalisation, governance-related crime, organised criminal activity, and the use of violence. Although these themes vary in their prominence and operationalisation across studies, together they illustrate the different ways in which kinship is understood as relevant to the structure and organisation of criminal activity. The review thus provides a conceptual synthesis of recurring dimensions in the literature, without suggesting that these dimensions are exhaustive or necessarily present in every KBOC network.

---

## 101. [Image Rights Infringements: Is the Common Law of Delict Sufficient or is It Time for Legislative Intervention?](<https://doi.org/10.17159/1727-3781/2026/v29i0a21812>)

- 作者：Layckan Gensen
- 发表日期：2026-11-09
- 引用次数：0
- DOI：https://doi.org/10.17159/1727-3781/2026/v29i0a21812
- [打开 PDF](<https://perjournal.co.za/article/download/21812/27515>)

**摘要：**

In modern commerce one could argue that the image of an individual constitutes an asset with a substantial intrinsic commercial value that may be used by third parties to generate profit, because the image of individuals is highly exploitable for commercial purposes (specifically in the context of the endorsement, marketing and advertising of brands, products and services). It is common practice for celebrities (and social media influencers alike) to engage in the marketing, merchandising and commercial exploitation of their own image for commercial gain, especially on various social media channels. Since the introduction of these channels it has become a business tool for individuals to generate an income by exploiting their image online. Despite this development and evolution of the use of social media channels, image rights and the protection thereof have not gained much attention in South Africa over the last few years. This raises the question, when an image rights infringement occurs, for example on social media, and an individual cannot rely on contractual remedies to rectify the breach (since there is no valid contract in place to regulate the use), what remedies are available to the individual, who has suffered patrimonial harm for the unauthorised use of their image. Case law has identified the common law of delict as an area of law that may provide plaintiffs in image rights infringement matters with possible relief for the financial loss they suffered due to the unauthorised use of their image. Thus, this article will investigate the common law of delict to determine whether it provides the most appropriate remedy to address image rights infringements outside a contractual agreement, by analysing South Africa case law in this regard.

## 103. [Digital Behavioral Analysis, Computer Crime, and Investigations: Persona ex Machina](<https://doi.org/10.4324/9781003671305>)

- 作者：C. R. Johnson
- 发表日期：2026-11-09
- 引用次数：0
- DOI：https://doi.org/10.4324/9781003671305
- [打开 PDF](<https://api.taylorfrancis.com/content/books/mono/download?identifierName=doi&identifierValue=10.4324/9781003671305&type=googlepdf>)

**摘要：**

Digital Behavioral Analysis, Computer Crime, and Investigations integrates digital forensic methodology with behavioral profiling, criminological theory, and the legal and ethical frameworks that govern admissibility for instruction and practice in equal measure. Behind every digital crime is a human being, and the technical sophistication of modern computer crime has not eliminated the behavioral evidence that has always grounded criminal investigation – where that evidence lives and how investigators recognize it has simply changed. From the Silk Road takedown to the Bangladesh Bank heist, from the Stagg investigation to the Hong Kong deepfake fraud, this book works through the cases that define the field and develops the frameworks that practitioners need to navigate the next generation of digital offenses. Each chapter opens with learning objectives and a key terms section that defines technical vocabulary in non-specialist terms, develops its analytic content through case illustrations drawn from documented investigations, and closes with practical takeaways and discussion questions calibrated for both classroom use and field application. C. R. Johnson establishes operational frameworks for evidence triage, hypothesis development, behavioral cluster analysis, case linkage analysis, and capability assessment, and treats each within the constraints of Federal Rule of Evidence 702 and the Daubert standard for expert testimony. Written for advanced undergraduate and graduate students in criminal justice, criminology, digital forensics, and cybersecurity programs, as well as for practitioners working in digital investigation, behavioral analysis, and the prosecution or defense of computer crime cases, this book is usable as both a course text and an active investigative reference.

## 104. [A Comparative Analysis of the Fintech Industry and Regulatory Frameworks in Indonesia and Malaysia](<https://doi.org/10.65638/2978-8196.2026.2.06>)

- 作者：Rafisah Mat Radzi
- 发表日期：2026-11-09
- 引用次数：0
- DOI：https://doi.org/10.65638/2978-8196.2026.2.06
- [打开 PDF](<https://iifssystem.com/index.php/iifss/article/download/33/18>)

**摘要：**

The rapid growth of financial technology (fintech), particularly in digital payment services, has revolutionized financial transactions worldwide. Across Asia, Malaysia and Indonesia have emerged as key players in harnessing fintech to enhance financial inclusion, improve efficiency, and modernize their economies. Although they share these objectives, the two countries’ development trajectories and regulatory frameworks differ substantially because of variations in their economic structures, levels of technological adoption, and regulatory practices. This study examines the fintech industries of Malaysia and Indonesia, with particular emphasis on their digital payment ecosystems, prevalent payment methods, and regulatory approaches, including frameworks for Islamic fintech. Drawing on an extensive review of academic literature and regulatory policies, this comparative analysis provides insights into strategic decisions that both countries could consider to enhance their fintech ecosystems and regulatory frameworks. The findings may serve as a guide for Indonesian and Malaysian policymakers and industry stakeholders seeking to establish resilient and inclusive fintech ecosystems supported by robust regulatory frameworks.

## 105. [The Thirsty Machine: India's AI Data Centre Race, the Water Crisis, and the Choice Between Argentina's Libertarian Gamble and China's Governance Blueprint](<https://doi.org/10.21203/rs.3.rs-10925216/v1>)

- 作者：Syed Mujtaba Athar; Hina Iliyas
- 发表日期：2026-11-09
- 引用次数：0
- DOI：https://doi.org/10.21203/rs.3.rs-10925216/v1
- [打开 PDF](<https://www.researchsquare.com/article/rs-10925216/latest.pdf>)

**摘要：**

Abstract India is building an artificial intelligence data centre network, which is expected to grow to 15 GW by 2031, almost entirely in severely water-stressed cities. This digital growth is coming at a time when we estimate that by 2025 water use has reached 150 billion litres per year, and by 2030 it will increase to 358 billion litres per year, while experiencing aquifer depletion, water scarcity in cities, and no requirement for environmental disclosure Significantly, under the Environment Impact Assessment Notification 2006, gigawatt-scale computing facilities are classified under building construction (Category B2) and hence do not require a public hearing, disclosure of water source, or mandatory Power Usage Effectiveness (PUE) and Water Usage Effectiveness (WUE) benchmarking.. There is no national data centre policy in force since it has been in draft since 2020. This study employs a most-different systems design model (Przeworski & Teune, 1970) to compare three distinct governance paradigms for AI infrastructure: India's democratic vacuum, Argentina's libertarian deregulation, and China's authoritarian spatial planning. This article examines the libertarian vision of the zero regulation, non-liability, AI corporation, governance-free zones for hyperscale infrastructure espoused by Argentina's Milei government as a model for Third World approaches to international law as neo-colonial resource extraction. China's "East Data, West Computing" initiative offers an alternative state-led roadmap and includes the commercial scale deployment of underwater cooling, the spatial movement of data centers to the interior, the imposition of PUE/WUE standards, and the implementation of water pricing. Based on the insights of recent sustainability science, of hydrology, and of governance scholarship, this article calls for a rejection of the race-to-the-bottom deregulatory impulse and of authoritarian centralisation in India, in favour of a sovereign, democratic path, based on mandatory environmental benchmarks, spatial planning, community consent, and institutional accountability. India's freshwater resources constitute a public commons requiring democratic governance, not an unregulated subsidy for expropriation of the global AI infrastructure expansion.

---

## 106. [AI-Enabled Cryptocurrency Scams and Investor Vulnerability: Evidence on Scam Sophistication, Adoption, Financial Access and Losses](<https://doi.org/10.21203/rs.3.rs-11383134/v1>)

- 作者：Sahila Chellamuthu; Mcxin Tee
- 发表日期：2026-11-09
- 引用次数：0
- DOI：https://doi.org/10.21203/rs.3.rs-11383134/v1
- [打开 PDF](<https://www.researchsquare.com/article/rs-11383134/latest.pdf>)

**摘要：**

Abstract Cryptocurrency fraud is among the largest categories of financial crime, and generative AI may be raising the scale and persuasiveness of scams. We link AI-enabled scam sophistication, cryptocurrency adoption and financial access to investor losses, using public blockchain-analytics, survey and World Bank data. Scam operations linked to AI tooling earned about 4.5 times more than other operations, a descriptive comparison. Across eleven countries, loss prevalence ranges from 1% to 36%, and exposure alone does not explain the gap. Crypto adoption is unrelated to losses (Spearman ρ = 0.21; interval − 0.46 to 0.74). Account ownership is negatively associated with losses (ρ = −0.74), but is confounded with income and region. We discuss implications for regulators, exchanges and AI providers.

---

## 107. [Usage of blockchain technology and shareholder-oriented firm performance: Evidence from Indian listed firms](<https://doi.org/10.56879/ijbm.v5i4.174>)

- 作者：Dikshita Thakkar; Himanshu
- 发表日期：2026-11-09
- 引用次数：0
- DOI：https://doi.org/10.56879/ijbm.v5i4.174
- [打开 PDF](<https://iessociety.org/index.php/IJBM/article/download/451/241>)

**摘要：**

Blockchain technology (BCT) is increasingly used to improve transparency and reduce information asymmetry within firms, yet evidence on how its usage affects shareholder-oriented firm performance (SFP) remains scarce, especially in emerging economies. This study examines whether usage of blockchain technology is associated with SFP among non-financial firms listed on the National Stock Exchange of India from 2016 to 2024. Usage is measured as a time varying binary indicator drawn from Compustat Global database and validated through textual analysis of annual reports. SFP is measured using the price to book value ratio, earnings per share, and the dividend payout ratio. A two-step system generalized method of moments estimator is used to address dynamic persistence and endogeneity, based on 3,501 firm year observations for 389 firms using BCT. The results show that usage of blockchain technology is positively associated with all the three measures. The association is significant at the 5 percent level for earnings per share and at the 10 percent level for the price to book value ratio and the dividend payout ratio. The study contributes to the fintech and corporate finance literature by providing multi-indicator evidence from an emerging market. It also offers implications for managers, investors, and policymakers who evaluate the financial returns to blockchain investment. The findings should be interpreted with caution given the modest significance levels and the binary measure of BCT usage.

## 109. [Examining the return dynamics of Bitcoin and Ethereum around major international football tournaments](<https://doi.org/10.21511/imfi.23(4).2026.16>)

- 作者：Wendy Wendy; Hansen Tandra; Mochammad Ridwan Ristyawan
- 发表日期：2026-11-09
- 引用次数：0
- DOI：https://doi.org/10.21511/imfi.23(4).2026.16
- [打开 PDF](<https://businessperspectives.org/images/pdf/applications/publishing/templates/article/assets/25563/IMFI_2026_04_Wendy.pdf>)

**摘要：**

Type of the article: Research ArticleAbstractCryptocurrencies have become increasingly important alternative investment assets, yet their returns remain sensitive to market conditions and external developments. Major international football tournaments attract substantial global attention, but evidence on whether these periods coincide with systematic changes in cryptocurrency returns remains limited. This study examines the return dynamics of Bitcoin and Ethereum before, during, and after major international football tournaments using monthly data from May 2016 to December 2025. Tournament-specific OLS regressions with market controls, calendar and trend adjustments, Newey–West HAC inference, and Benjamini-Hochberg FDR correction were employed in this study. The results show substantial heterogeneity across tournaments, cryptocurrencies, and temporal phases. Although several tournament-window coefficients are nominally significant, broader FDR adjustment substantially narrows the supported findings. The strongest evidence is concentrated around the FIFA World Cup, with a negative association between the tournament period and Bitcoin returns, and a negative associations between the pre-tournament periods and Ethereum returns. International football tournament periods are not uniformly associated with cryptocurrency returns, and the observed patterns depend on the specific asset, tournament phase, and event context. These findings are consistent with a potential behavioral or investor-attention mechanism, although attention and sentiment are not directly measured.

Methodology documentation for the Machine Economy Index (MEI) and its legal component, the Legal Rail Readiness Score (LRRS), methodology version 1.3 (20 October 2026). The record contains the conceptual and mathematical specification of the index framework, inclusion criteria, normalization bounds methodology, legal-coverage scoring rules, and versioning protocol. The MEI tracks machine-economy activity across payments, physical infrastructure, and legal readiness, plus a macro demand component, combined with a geometric mean. The LRRS measures GDP-weighted legal coverage across 48 jurisdictions and five categories. The public methodology overview is available at https://machineeconomy.ai/methodology. ## 111. [Conditional Integration of AI Equities with Traditional and Alternative Assets: Evidence from Wavelet Coherence and Time-Varying Connectedness](<https://doi.org/10.3390/jrfm19110907>)

- 作者：Heni Boubaker; Nadia Farjallah
- 发表日期：2026-11-09
- 引用次数：0
- DOI：https://doi.org/10.3390/jrfm19110907

**摘要：**

This study examines whether the equities of leading artificial intelligence (AI) firms were integrated with Bitcoin, gold, West Texas Intermediate (WTI) crude oil and clean energy from January 2018 to July 2026. Using an equally weighted index of NVIDIA, Apple, Alphabet and Microsoft, we combine wavelet coherence, time-varying parameter vector autoregressive (TVP-VAR) connectedness and time-varying Granger causality, complemented by windowed scalogram difference and variance decomposition analyses. Dependence is time-varying and horizon-dependent: significant co-movements concentrate around the COVID-19 crash, the 2022 inflation and monetary tightening shock, and the AI-driven boom. Average total connectedness was low (20.23%) but exceeded 50% in March 2020. Clean energy and the AI index are net transmitters of shocks, whereas Bitcoin, gold and oil are net receivers; however, much of the transmitting role of AI equities reflects their exposure to the broad equity market. Lagged predictability is episodic, and AI equities are almost never predicted by the other assets. The results are robust to ex ante AI exchange-traded funds, alternative model specifications, weekly data and Brent oil. AI equities therefore appear only conditionally integrated with other asset classes, and diversification benefits observed in calm periods should not be assumed to persist during stress episodes.

---

## 113. [РЕГРЕСІЙНО-СЦЕНАРНЕ МОДЕЛЮВАННЯ ДОВГОСТРОКОВОЇ ДИНАМІКИ КАПІТАЛІЗАЦІЇ СВІТОВОГО КРИПТОВАЛЮТНОГО РИНКУ: МЕТОДОЛОГІЧНІ ОБМЕЖЕННЯ ЕКСТРАПОЛЯЦІЙНИХ ПІДХОДІВ](<https://doi.org/10.32782/2522-1256-2026-50-18>)

- 作者：Mariana Myronova; Юлія Полякова
- 发表日期：2026-11-09
- 引用次数：0
- DOI：https://doi.org/10.32782/2522-1256-2026-50-18
- [打开 PDF](<https://journals-lute.lviv.ua/index.php/pidpr-torgi/article/download/2495/2338>)

**摘要：**

У статті досліджено методологічні можливості й обмеження регресійно-екстраполяційного підходу до прогнозування довгострокової динаміки капіталізації світового криптовалютного ринку. Актуальність теми зумовлена стрімким і водночас вкрай нерівномірним зростанням крипторинку упродовж 2011-2025 рр., що ускладнює застосування традиційних трендових моделей для обґрунтування прогнозних і сценарних оцінок, необхідних суб’єктам зовнішньоекономічної діяльності та органам державної політики. На основі статистичного ряду капіталізації крипторинку за 2011-2025 рр., сформованого за даними провідних міжнародних аналітичних платформ, побудовано логарифмічно-лінійну регресійну модель методом найменших квадратів, отримано статистично значуще рівняння з коефіцієнтом детермінації 0,866 та значущим на рівні 1% коефіцієнтом нахилу. Емпірично доведено, що механічна екстраполяція моделі, побудованої на довгому ряді з фазою вибухового зростання від низької бази, генерує вкрай широкі довірчі інтервали прогнозу, які на практиці роблять точкові оцінки на кілька років уперед статистично необґрунтованими, а подекуди економічно абсурдними. Порівняльний аналіз регресії на повній вибірці (2011-2025 рр.) і скороченій вибірці зрілого ринку (2016-2025 рр.) підтвердив гіпотезу про уповільнення темпів зростання капіталізації в міру дозрівання ринку, що узгоджується з логікою S-подібної траєкторії дифузії фінансових інновацій та зростанням довіри учасників ринку в міру накопичення практичного досвіду використання криптоактивів. Обґрунтовано доцільність доповнення регресійного інструментарію сценарним підходом, що враховує альтернативні комбінації макроекономічних, регуляторних і технологічних факторів невизначеності та спирається на систематизацію актуальних прогнозів провідних фінансових інституцій світу. Результати дослідження мають практичне значення для формування адаптивних, а не точкових стратегій суб’єктів зовнішньоекономічної діяльності та регуляторної політики за умов високої волатильності криптовалютного ринку.

---

## 114. [Machine Learning for Money Laundering Analysis](<https://doi.org/10.53941/ijndi.2026.100024>)

- 作者：Victor I. C. Chang; Hai Wang; Siddharth Raut; Assel Akzhalova; Weihang Huang
- 发表日期：2026-11-09
- 引用次数：0
- DOI：https://doi.org/10.53941/ijndi.2026.100024
- [打开 PDF](<https://media.sciltp.com/articles/2611005618/2611005618.pdf>)

**摘要：**

This study investigates the application of machine learning (ML) for proactive threat hunting in financial transaction systems, focusing on detecting money laundering activities—a critical challenge in business cybersecurity. Utilizing the Elliptic Bitcoin dataset, we apply six supervised ML models: Random Forest, Support Vector Machine, K-Nearest Neighbors, Stochastic Gradient Descent, Decision Tree, and Logistic Regression. We address class imbalance using the Synthetic Minority Oversampling Technique (SMOTE) and conduct exploratory data analysis to identify key transactional features. Evaluated using cybersecurity-relevant metrics (accuracy, precision, recall, F1-score), the Random Forest classifier outperforms others, achieving 98.87% accuracy on the holdout set. This work contributes an empirical benchmarking study demonstrating that, on the Elliptic Bitcoin dataset, Random Forest achieves the highest macro-F1 (0.965) and ROC-AUC (0.996) under random-split evaluation, while also revealing substantial performance degradation for all classifiers under temporally ordered evaluation—a finding with direct implications for realistic AML deployment.

## 115. [Post-trade Exposure Dispersion: Participant Composition and Calendar Weighting in a Transparent Perpetual Market (Companion Code and Aggregate Results)](<https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.24086044>)

- 作者：Bolang Fang
- 发表日期：2026-11-09
- 引用次数：0
- DOI：https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.24086044

**摘要：**

This release contains companion code and aggregate results for a study of post-trade exposure dispersion in the Hyperliquid BTC perpetual market. The study follows the passive recipient addresses in each trade episode and measures changes in their signed BTC exposure over the next 30 seconds. The evaluation covers 48,636 source-supported episodes across 14 dates in December 2025, using selected UTC hours. The package includes Python scripts for verifying the saved results, a source-file inventory, and research implementation files. Run python -B verify.py from the repository root to verify the distributed materials. The raw trade files and private event-level ledgers are not included. The original data are available from Albers et al. at https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.18184441.

## 116. [Bitcoin: The Little Rule That Beats the Market](<https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.24092036>)

- 作者：Manuel Amon Bossi
- 发表日期：2026-11-09
- 引用次数：0
- DOI：https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.24092036

**摘要：**

I study a daily rule that sets Bitcoin exposure from two observable inputs: the trend of a foreign-currency basket against the dollar and the deviation of Bitcoin's price from a fixed power-law curve. I construct the basket from the Japanese yen, Chinese yuan and British pound, using Federal Reserve trade weights published at each annual reset. In this paper, the market means Bitcoin buy-and-hold. From January 1, 2014 to November 6, 2026, the rule earned a compound annual growth rate (CAGR) of 70.41%, a Sharpe ratio of 1.46, a maximum drawdown of -51.91% and a final multiple of 941.64 times. Buy-and-hold earned 43.19%, 0.87, -83.62% and 100.63 times. The results are a historical evaluation. Paired 95% bootstrap intervals include zero for the CAGR and Sharpe gaps and exclude zero for the drawdown gap. I selected the currencies and parameters with knowledge of this sample, and the account uses synthetic short positions and omits financing costs. I motivate the rule with the hypothesis that easier global dollar conditions support portfolio demand for Bitcoin. The backtest leaves that channel unidentified, and the rule needs a prospective holdout.
